- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43045649/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-18

#### 摘要

Our previous studies have shown that Huanglian Jiedu Decoction effectively suppresses central immune-inflammatory responses, modulates peripheral inflammatory mediators, reduces the infiltration of peripheral T lymphocytes into the central nervous system, and promotes the conversion of infiltrated T lymphocytes in the brain into Treg cells. However, whether Huanglian Jiedu Decoction can influence M2 microglial polarization by regulating T-lymphocyte phenotypic transformation and thereby modulate neuroinflammation after ischemic stroke remains unclear. This study focused on Treg-mediated microglial polarization to investigate the potential of Huanglian Jiedu Decoction to modulate interactions between T lymphocytes and microglia in the treatment of ischemic stroke. We established a rat model of middle cerebral artery occlusion, an inflammatory model of Jurkat T cells stimulated with phorbol 12-myristate 13-acetate and ionomycin, and a Transwell co-culture model of T lymphocytes and HMC3 microglia. The results showed that Huanglian Jiedu Decoction effectively alleviated cerebral ischemia-reperfusion injury in a rat model of middle cerebral artery, inhibited central inflammatory responses, and promoted the polarization of microglia toward the M2 phenotype. Huanglian Jiedu Decoction also promoted the conversion of CD4+ T cells in brain tissue into regulatory T cells and regulated regulatory T cells through the interleukin-10/Janus kinase 1/signal transducer and activator of transcription 3 pathway, thereby promoting the activation of M2 microglia. These findings suggest that Huanglian Jiedu Decoction, which has heat-clearing and detoxifying properties, can regulate immune homeostasis, inhibit secondary neuroinflammation associated with ischemic stroke, and exert neuroprotective effects. Huanglian Jiedu Decoction may therefore treat ischemic stroke by modulating the interaction between T lymphocytes and microglia, providing a traditional Chinese medicine-based strategy for the treatment of ischemic stroke.

### [C] Neuroprotective effects of ropinirole against nicotine- and ethanol-induced cognitive impairment, oxidative stress, neuroinflammation, and neurodegenerative alterations in rats.

- PMID: 43045009
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence

- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Life sciences
- 作者: Azizi O, Ghasemi R, Kakhki S, Etebari M, Sabet K, Beheshti F
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 17
- DOI: 10.1016/j.lfs.2026.124789
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43045009/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-18

#### 摘要

BACKGROUND: Adolescence is a critical period of brain development during which exposure to addictive substances may adversely affect cognitive function and neuronal plasticity. Combined nicotine and ethanol exposure may contribute to persistent cognitive impairment through disturbances in oxidative balance, inflammatory signaling, and hippocampal function. However, the potential effects of pharmacological interventions targeting these alterations after exposure remain insufficiently characterized. Given the previously reported neuroprotective properties of ropinirole, this study investigated whether post-exposure treatment with this dopamine D2/D3 receptor agonist could attenuate cognitive deficits and associated hippocampal biochemical alterations in rats subjected to adolescent nicotine-ethanol co-exposure.
METHODS: Male Wistar rats were exposed to nicotine (2 mg/kg/day, intraperitoneally) and ethanol (20% v/v in drinking water) during adolescence (postnatal days 21-48). Following the exposure period, animals received oral ropinirole at doses of 5, 10, or 20 mg/kg/day for three weeks. Spatial learning and memory and recognition memory were assessed using the Morris water maze and novel object recognition tests, respectively. Hippocampal biochemical assessments included malondialdehyde (MDA), nitrite, total thiol content, superoxide dismutase (SOD), catalase, tumor necrosis factor-α (TNF-α), interleukin-10 (IL-10), brain-derived neurotrophic factor (BDNF), acetylcholinesterase (AChE), and amyloid-β (Aβ).
RESULTS: Nicotine-ethanol co-exposure significantly impaired spatial learning, memory retention, and recognition memory, accompanied by increased hippocampal MDA, nitrite, TNF-α, AChE activity, and Aβ levels, together with reduced antioxidant capacity, IL-10, and BDNF expression. Post-exposure ropinirole treatment improved performance in the behavioral tests and attenuated several of the associated biochemical alterations. The 20 mg/kg dose generally showed the most pronounced effects among the doses tested; however, a formal dose-response relationship was not established.
CONCLUSION: These findings suggest that ropinirole exerts neuroprotective effects against adolescent nicotine-ethanol-induced cognitive impairment through the modulation of oxidative stress, neuroinflammation, cholinergic dysfunction, neurotrophic signaling, and amyloid-related pathways. These results provide preliminary evidence supporting dopaminergic modulation as a potential strategy for mitigating substance-associated cognitive dysfunction, although further mechanistic and translational studies are necessary.

- DOI: 10.1016/j.brainresbull.2026.112221
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43044685/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-18

#### 摘要

This study integrated multi-omics data and machine learning to prioritize candidate plasma proteins and to predict the multi-target interactions of traditional Chinese medicine (TCM) in Alzheimer's disease (AD). Mendelian randomization across three cohorts (Icelandic, UK Biobank, and Fenland) identified 66 AD-associated plasma proteins, including TREM2, ACE, GRN, and CR1, with TREM2 showing a robust protective effect (OR = 0.78, 95% CI: 0.75-0.81). Single-cell RNA sequencing showed neuron-specific downregulation of NSF and TMEM106B in AD, implicating synaptic dysfunction and impaired axonal transport. A machine-learning classifier (K-nearest neighbors, KNN) incorporating TREM2, TMEM106B, C1S, PLOD2, and NSF, trained on brain-tissue expression data, demonstrated moderate predictive performance (AUC = 0.76, 95% CI: 0.63-0.90) and is presented as a candidate brain-tissue-derived classifier rather than a validated non-invasive plasma diagnostic panel. Immune infiltration analysis revealed altered M2 macrophage and plasma cell dynamics, with NSF negatively correlated with γδ T-cell infiltration (p < 0.001). Network pharmacology prioritized five candidate TCM herbs (e.g., Scutellaria baicalensis [Huang Qin] and Alpinia oxyphylla [Yi Zhi]) and bioactive compounds (stigmasterol and quercetin) targeting AD-related proteins. Molecular docking predicted strong binding between stigmasterol and PLOD2 (binding energy: -11.2kcal/mol), suggesting a potential role in modulating extracellular matrix remodeling. These compounds were enriched in the PI3K-Akt and NF-κB pathways, consistent with the anti-inflammatory effects observed in TCM formulations such as GHYSJ. These findings link candidate biomarker discovery with the predicted multi-target interactions of TCM, providing hypotheses for future non-invasive diagnosis and precision therapy in AD.

### [C] Precision immunopharmacology in xenotransplantation: integrating genome-engineered donors, organoid testbeds, and AI-guided translation.

- PMID: 43044535
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Exploratory
- 转化相关性依据: Cross-domain vascular mechanism with exploratory CCM relevance
- 优先级依据: C: broad, exploratory, low-relevance, or unmatched article
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: International immunopharmacology
- 作者: Ren T, Fu G, Duan S, Zeng M, Gao Y
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 17
- DOI: 10.1016/j.intimp.2026.117691
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43044535/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-18

#### 摘要

Xenotransplantation is moving from isolated proof-of-concept procedures toward an engineered biotechnology platform for organ replacement. This review examines how genome-designed donor pigs, recipient-directed immunopharmacology, human-relevant in vitro testbeds, and data-driven decision systems can be integrated to address the remaining barriers to clinical translation. CRISPR-Cas-based engineering enables deletion of dominant carbohydrate xenoantigens, insertion of human complement- and coagulation-regulatory genes, modulation of innate and adaptive immune checkpoints, control of donor-organ growth, and inactivation of porcine endogenous retrovirus sequences. However, donor engineering does not eliminate residual T-cell, B-cell, antibody, complement, natural killer (NK) cell, macrophage, and inflammatory responses. Contemporary immunopharmacological strategies therefore increasingly combine costimulation blockade with complement inhibition, lymphocyte-directed therapy, and selected cytokine- or innate-immune interventions, with future regimens requiring closer integration of pharmacokinetic and pharmacodynamic monitoring with donor genotype and organ-specific risk. Organoids and microfluidic vascular systems may provide an intermediate testing layer by quantifying antibody binding, complement activation, endothelial injury, coagulation, leukocyte transmigration, cytokine responses, and tissue-specific function under controlled conditions before escalation to large-animal studies. Artificial intelligence may further support xenoantigen discovery, genome-editing design, donor-recipient matching, multimodal rejection surveillance, and ultimately clinician-supervised immunopharmacological decision support. We propose that these components should be connected through a traceable design-build-test-learn workflow linking computational prediction, donor construction, standardized functional testing, non-human-primate validation, and early human data. Durable translation will depend not on any single technological advance, but on reproducible donor manufacturing, precision immune control, validated human-relevant test systems, standardized longitudinal data, pathogen surveillance, organ-specific management, and transparent regulatory governance.

### [C] Targeting Energy Metabolism and Tryptophan Pathways in Alzheimer's Disease for Developing Therapeutic Strategies.

- PMID: 43043577
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low
- 转化相关性依据: Only generic or out-of-scope keyword overlap

- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Molecular neurobiology
- 作者: Joshi D, Uniyal S, Singh SK, Kollur SP, Singh P
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 17
- DOI: 10.1007/s12035-026-06340-7
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43043577/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-18

#### 摘要

Alzheimer's disease (AD) is the primary cause of dementia worldwide and carries a high socioeconomic burden. However, despite the fact that such pathological features as amyloid-beta plaques and neurofibrillary tangles are considered hallmark traits of AD, therapeutic approaches targeting these pathological features have shown limited clinical efficacy to date. Recent scientific discoveries have drawn attention to the importance of metabolic processes preceding neurodegeneration. Reduced glucose utilization and impaired oxidative phosphorylation (OXPHOS) are associated with bioenergetic deficits that may increase neuronal vulnerability to damage. In addition, neuroinflammation is associated with altered tryptophan metabolism through the kynurenine pathway and may favor the production of potentially neurotoxic metabolites. Despite these separate aspects of AD, the interaction between impaired bioenergetics and altered tryptophan metabolism in the brain has not been sufficiently studied. This gap complicates the development of multi-targeted treatment strategies. The purpose of this narrative review is to examine how gut dysbiosis, immune activation, mitochondrial oxidative phosphorylation (OXPHOS) impairment, redox imbalance, and tryptophan-kynurenine pathway dysregulation interact and may contribute to AD development and progression, including associated neuropsychiatric symptoms. We examine whether NAD⁺ depletion and rerouting of tryptophan metabolism may act as key nodes linking mitochondrial respiratory failure with the generation of neurotoxic kynurenine metabolites, excitotoxic glutamatergic signaling, loss of neurotrophic support, and synaptic dysfunction, and to analyze existing and emerging therapeutic strategies that target these interconnected pathways. In addition, this review highlights potentially promising interventions, such as NAD⁺ precursor supplementation, central nervous system insulin delivery, and modification of gut microbiome. Here we propose that the energy-tryptophan metabolic axis is a promising but underexplored area of AD research. Targeting these convergent bioenergetic and metabolomic pathways may support precision intervention strategies that could extend beyond conventional amyloid-centric paradigms to address broader neurodegenerative and neuropsychiatric syndromes.

### [C] Mechanisms of traditional Chinese medicine-derived active components in the treatment of Alzheimer's disease: A comprehensive review.

- PMID: 42933739
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low
- 转化相关性依据: Only generic or out-of-scope keyword overlap
- 优先级依据: C: broad, exploratory, low-relevance, or unmatched article
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 经典补位
- 期刊: Journal of ethnopharmacology
- 作者: Tian Y, Guo J, Lin X, Zhang F, Xie J, Hou X, et al.
- 发表日期: 2027 Feb 10
- DOI: 10.1016/j.jep.2026.122574
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42933739/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-19

#### 摘要

ETHNOPHARMACOLOGICAL RELEVANCE: Alzheimer's disease (AD) is a neurodegenerative disorder characterized by progressive cognitive decline and multifactorial pathology. Because AD involves interacting pathological processes, the limited efficacy of conventional single-target therapies has increased interest in traditional Chinese medicine (TCM) and other natural medicines with multi-target pharmacological actions. TCM-derived active components originate from medicinal materials used in traditional practice and are evaluated using modern pharmacological approaches. Their source medicines have diverse traditional-use backgrounds. Some have historical associations with memory or cognitive symptoms, whereas many others were traditionally used for broader deficiency, phlegm, blood-stasis, heat, or related syndromic patterns. These records provide ethnopharmacological context for the source medicines but should not be taken as historical evidence for the anti-AD activity of their isolated constituents.
AIM OF THE REVIEW: This review synthesizes current evidence on the mechanisms of TCM-derived bioactive components in AD while incorporating relevant traditional-use information on their source medicines. Particular attention is given to their multi-target actions and emerging mechanisms involving ferroptosis, mitophagy, and the gut-brain axis.
MATERIALS AND METHODS: Mechanistic literature published between January 2019 and November 2025 was retrieved from Ovid MEDLINE, PubMed, ScienceDirect, and Web of Science. Ovid MEDLINE and PubMed were searched separately to maximize retrieval across indexed and additional PubMed records, with overlapping records removed during deduplication. Traditional-use information was evaluated separately for the source medicines. A targeted search of PubMed and ClinicalTrials.gov was also conducted to assess human interventional evidence for compounds covered in the review, with trial-registration information updated through August 2026.
RESULTS: Current preclinical findings suggest that TCM active components may produce coordinated preclinical pharmacological effects through multiple pathways, including the inhibition of β-amyloid (Aβ) production and deposition, regulation of tau aggregation and phosphorylation, alleviation of neuroinflammation and oxidative stress, improvement of mitophagy and iron metabolism disorders, and modulation of gut microbiota homeostasis. Among these components, saponins show broad mechanistic coverage. Flavonoids, polysaccharides, and other bioactive substances have likewise shown anti-inflammatory and antioxidant activities, together with protective effects on synaptic function, in preclinical models. These mechanistic findings were derived predominantly from cellular and animal studies, with computational analyses providing mainly hypothesis-generating support. Human evidence was available for only a small subset of compounds and was not uniformly positive. Huperzine A has progressed to controlled clinical testing but has shown mixed efficacy findings; resveratrol demonstrated CNS exposure and biomarker activity without consistent cognitive benefit; curcumin did not show efficacy in a placebo-controlled AD trial; and favorable signals from ginseng studies were limited by open-label designs and the use of whole preparations rather than isolated ginsenosides.
CONCLUSIONS: The available evidence supports broad preclinical activity of TCM-derived components across several interconnected AD-related processes, but the strength of mechanistic support varies markedly among compounds and pathways. Human translation remains limited and inconsistent, and current clinical data do not establish disease-modifying efficacy for the compounds reviewed. Further development should therefore be guided by the main uncertainty surrounding each candidate-whether mechanistic causality, reproducibility, systemic or CNS exposure, preparation standardization, or clinical efficacy-rather than by a fixed sequence of technologies.

### [C] Neuroprotective effects of quercetin in animal models of neurodegenerative diseases: A systematic review and meta-analysis.

- PMID: 42121268
- Domain: Out-of-scope

- 来源类型: 经典补位
- 期刊: Journal of the science of food and agriculture
- 作者: Cho IH, Putra HM, Jung CW, Hyun GH, Jang HH, Hwang IG, et al.
- 发表日期: 2027 Jan 15
- DOI: 10.1002/jsfa.70699
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42121268/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-19

#### 摘要

Neurodegenerative conditions such as Alzheimer's disease and Parkinson's disease are characterized by progressive neuronal loss driven by oxidative stress and inflammation. Quercetin, a dietary flavonoid with established antioxidant and anti-inflammatory properties, has emerged as a potential neuroprotective agent. This study aimed to quantitatively synthesize and evaluate preclinical evidence regarding the impact of quercetin on neurodegenerative biomarkers and cognitive performance. A comprehensive literature search was conducted using PubMed, Embase, Web of Science, and the Cochrane Central Register of Controlled Trials (CENTRAL). After strict screening and selection, 19 studies were included. These evaluated the effects of quercetin on: Morris water maze (MWM) performance; inflammatory cytokines - including interleukin-6 (IL-6), tumor necrosis factor-alpha (TNF-α), and interleukin-10 (IL-10); the oxidative stress marker malondialdehyde (MDA); antioxidant enzymes such as catalase (CAT), superoxide dismutase (SOD), and glutathione (GSH); brain-derived neurotrophic factor (BDNF); and acetylcholinesterase (AChE) activity. Subgroup analyses based on quercetin dose (<100 mg kg⁻¹ versus ≥100 mg kg⁻¹) and treatment duration (<28 versus ≥28 days) were performed. Quercetin improved cognitive performance significantly by reducing escape latency and improving performance on memory retention indicators. It decreased pro-inflammatory cytokines (IL-6 and TNF-α), increased IL-10, enhanced antioxidant enzyme activity (CAT, SOD, and GSH), reduced MDA levels, up-regulated BDNF, and inhibited AChE. Subgroup analyses suggested that quercetin exerted stronger effects at lower doses and with longer treatment durations, although not all subgroup differences were statistically significant. Quercetin demonstrated multi-targeted neuroprotective effects in animal models, improving cognition and modulating inflammatory, oxidative, and neurotrophic pathways. These findings support the potential of quercetin as a therapeutic agent for neurodegenerative diseases, warranting further clinical investigation. © 2026 The Author(s). Journal of the Science of Food and Agriculture published by John Wiley & Sons Ltd on behalf of Society of Chemical Industry.

### [C] Reprogramming the tumor microenvironment in MSS CRC: the role of TKIs in combination with immunotherapy.

- PMID: 43032263

- 来源类型: 经典补位
- 期刊: ESMO gastrointestinal oncology
- 作者: Venturini J, Rossini D, Conticello C, Guarino A, Motola C, Maceria M, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Dec
- DOI: 10.1016/j.esmogo.2026.100439
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43032263/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-19

#### 摘要

Immunotherapy has revolutionized the treatment landscape of colorectal cancer (CRC), improving clinical outcomes in microsatellite instability-high tumors. In contrast, microsatellite stable (MSS) CRC exhibits intrinsic resistance to immune checkpoint inhibitors (ICIs), driven by reduced neoantigen load, resulting in an immunologically 'cold' tumor microenvironment (TME). To overcome these barriers, combination strategies integrating ICIs with tyrosine kinase inhibitors (TKIs) have been investigated based on their ability to inhibit multiple oncogenic and angiogenic signaling pathways. Beyond their antiangiogenic effects, preclinical evidence has demonstrated that TKIs can induce immunological reprogramming of the TME, promoting proinflammatory macrophage polarization, antigen presentation, vascular normalization, and T-cell infiltration. This evidence supported several early-phase clinical trials evaluating TKI-ICI combinations, showing encouraging signals of clinical activity in refractory MSS CRC. Recently, the interim analysis of the phase III STELLAR-303 trial demonstrated an overall survival benefit with the combination of zanzalintinib plus atezolizumab compared with regorafenib in pretreated MSS CRC. Notably, a consistent exploratory finding across the TKI-ICI trials was the potential role of liver metastases in driving local and systemic immunosuppression. Preclinical MSS CRC mouse models confirmed this suggestion, highlighting the association between the presence of liver metastases and impaired effector T-cell responses and antigen presentation. Overall, the heterogeneity of clinical outcomes underscores the complexity of immune resistance in MSS CRC and supports the development of multimodal biomarkers integrating spatial pathology, immune profiling, and artificial intelligence. This review discusses current preclinical and clinical evidence on TKI-ICI combinations in MSS CRC, exploring emerging mechanisms of immune resistance and potential therapeutic vulnerabilities.

- 作者: Porwal M, Malviya R, Chandra P, Sridhar SB, Shareef J, Wadhwa T, et al.

- DOI: 10.1016/j.jneuroim.2026.579099
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42732715/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-19

#### 摘要

Parkinson's disease (PD) is a progressive neurodegenerative disease that is associated with the loss of dopaminergic neurons and the formation of α-synuclein aggregates in the brain. Changes in the gut microbiota have been closely associated with PD pathophysiology and may contribute to disease-related processes through neuroinflammatory, metabolic, and immune-mediated mechanisms. This review explores the connection between the microbiome-gut-brain axis and Parkinson's disease and highlights new microbiome-based therapeutic interventions to help manage the disease. The extensive literature review was conducted using peer-reviewed articles from scientific databases such as Google Scholar, PubMed, and Scopus, and included articles concerned with opposing gut microbiota changes, mechanistic pathways, and microbiome-based targeted therapeutic strategies for PD. Existing data suggest that gut dysbiosis may contribute to mechanisms associated with PD pathogenesis through alterations in immune responses, α-synuclein aggregation, and neurotransmitter signalling. Restoration of microbial balance and alleviation of neurological and gastrointestinal symptoms are potential benefits of therapeutic interventions targeting microorganisms, including probiotics, prebiotics, dietary changes, and faecal microbiota transplantation (FMT). The microbiome-gut-brain axis is a promising approach to preventing and treating Parkinson's disease, but additional clinical trials are needed to determine its long-term effectiveness and safety.

### [C] Toward a conceptual framework for systemic effects of continuous renal replacement therapy in critical illness.

- PMID: 42661331
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context

- 转化相关性依据: Cross-domain vascular mechanism with exploratory CCM relevance

- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 经典补位
- 期刊: The International journal of artificial organs
- 作者: Nishimura Y
- 发表日期: 2026 Dec
- DOI: 10.1177/03913988261473824
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42661331/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-19

#### 摘要

Continuous renal replacement therapy (CRRT) is traditionally used for solute and fluid removal in critically ill patients with acute kidney injury. Emerging evidence suggests that CRRT may also influence systemic biological networks, including inflammation, vascular function, immunity, and metabolism. In sepsis and critical illness, these systems are dynamically dysregulated, involving fluctuating inflammatory mediators, endothelial alterations, immune dysfunction ranging from hyperinflammation to immunoparalysis, and metabolic reprogramming that varies with disease stage. We propose a conceptual framework in which CRRT modulates these interconnected processes. Beyond solute and cytokine removal, CRRT may affect the distribution of circulating mediators, attenuate concentration peaks, influence endothelial stability, and modulate immune and metabolic states, thereby contributing to systemic network regulation. However, CRRT has bidirectional effects. While it may help restore homeostasis by removing harmful mediators, it may also eliminate beneficial molecules and induce inflammatory or oxidative responses due to extracorporeal circulation. Therefore, its overall impact is likely context-dependent and influenced by treatment settings and patient condition. Although direct evidence for systemic reprogramming remains limited, this framework provides a basis for future research. Integrative biomarker-based and phenotyping approaches may support the development of individualized and adaptive CRRT strategies in critical care.

### [C] Neuroimmune mechanisms of the gut-brain axis in treatment-resistant depression: Implications for microbiome-based therapeutic strategies.

- PMID: 42561489
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low

- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 经典补位
- 期刊: Journal of neuroimmunology
- 作者: Khobragade R, Chaudhary A, Gautam Y, Ali MAM, Patil H, Kaushalye P, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Dec
- DOI: 10.1016/j.jneuroim.2026.579050
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42561489/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-19

#### 摘要

Treatment-resistant depression (TRD) represents a major clinical challenge characterised by inadequate response to conventional antidepressant therapies and high relapse rates. Emerging evidence suggests that TRD may extend beyond monoaminergic dysfunction and may involve dysregulation of the HPA axis, neuroinflammation, impaired neuroplasticity, and disruption of the gut-brain axis (GBA). Gut dysbiosis has been associated with treatment resistance through alterations in monoamine turnover, immune signalling, intestinal barrier integrity, and drug-microbiome interactions affecting antidepressant bioavailability. This review integrates emerging evidence supporting targeted modulation of the GBA as a mechanistically informed strategy for TRD. Specific microbial strains (e.g., Christensenella minuta, Akkermansia muciniphila, Bifidobacterium breve CCFM1025), microbial metabolites (e.g., indole-3-propionic acid, indole-3-lactic acid, anserine), and phytochemicals (e.g., curcumin, matrine, salidroside) are discussed for their proposed roles in modulating neuroendocrine signalling, neuroinflammation, and synaptic plasticity. The review also highlights emerging peripheral biomarkers, including the kynurenine/tryptophan ratio, serum metabolomics, and lymphocyte serotonin transporter clustering, as candidate tools for stratified psychiatry. Most of the evidence discussed in this review comes from animal studies, in vitro systems, and computational analyses, while direct evidence in patients with treatment-resistant depression remains limited. These findings provide important mechanistic insights into gut-brain axis dysfunction but require further validation in human TRD populations. Biomarker-guided and endotype-based approaches targeting the gut-brain axis may offer a useful framework for future research, although their clinical utility has yet to be established.

### [C] Neuronal IL-34 programs microglial activity via the TGF-β-MEF2C axis to constrain neuroinflammation following brain ischemia.

- PMID: 43050323
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence

- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Proceedings of the National Academy of Sciences of the United States of America
- 作者: Li M, Zhou X, Li R, Liu H, Qi C, Zhang W, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 24
- DOI: 10.1073/pnas.2613902123
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43050323/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-20

#### 摘要

Brain ischemia induces robust neuroinflammation driven by microglial activation, yet whether ischemic neurons actively reprogram microglial phenotypes to limit inflammation remains elusive. Here, we identify neuronal interleukin-34 (IL-34) as a critical immunomodulatory signal that maintains microglial homeostasis after cerebral ischemia. We demonstrate that ischemic injury augments IL-34-CSF1R signaling between neurons and microglia. Neuron-specific overexpression of Il34 (Syn1-Il34tg mice) attenuated infarct volume and neurodeficits after middle cerebral artery occlusion, whereas neuronal Il34 knockdown exacerbated brain injury. Microglial depletion abolished IL-34-mediated neuroprotection, suggesting microglia as essential effectors. Single-cell RNA sequencing revealed that neuronal IL-34 preserved homeostatic microglial signatures (P2ry12, Cx3cr1, Tgfbr1), while suppressing inflammatory activities (Il1b, Tnf, Cxcl2) through upregulation of the transcription factor MEF2C. Mechanistically, IL-34 activated TGF-β signaling in microglia and inhibition of TGF-β signaling abolished the protective effects of IL-34, whereas microglia-specific Mef2c overexpression rescued neuroprotection despite TGF-β blockade. Thus, ischemic neurons deploy IL-34 to orchestrate the immunoregulatory activity of microglia via the TGF-β-MEF2C axis, representing a previously unrecognized endogenous mechanism counteracting neuroinflammation. Our study reveals neuronal IL-34 as a potential therapeutic target for ischemic stroke.

### [C] Chemotherapy-induced mast-cell degranulation constrains tertiary lymphoid maturation in lung cancer brain metastasis.

- PMID: 43053209
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low
- 转化相关性依据: Only generic or out-of-scope keyword overlap
- 优先级依据: C: broad, exploratory, low-relevance, or unmatched article
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Journal of hematology & oncology
- 作者: Fu M, You M, Ao Y, Zeng G, Xing Y, Qiu F, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 18
- DOI: 10.1186/s13045-026-01869-5
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43053209/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-20

#### 摘要

BACKGROUND: Tertiary lymphoid structures (TLSs) support local adaptive immunity and are associated with favorable antitumor responses across multiple cancers. However, how anticancer treatment reshapes TLS maturation, particularly in lung cancer brain metastasis (LCBM), remains poorly understood.
METHODS: We integrated clinicopathological profiling of LCBM cohorts with bulk and single-cell RNA sequencing, spatial profiling, immune-repertoire analysis, histological validation, and murine intracranial tumor models. Associations between prior treatment and TLS maturation were evaluated using multivariable analyses. Mechanistic studies examined chemotherapy-associated IL-33 release, mast-cell activation, and the effects of PGD2 on antigen-specific T-cell cytotoxicity and B-cell differentiation. Pharmacological inhibition of H-PGDS and mast-cell stabilization were further evaluated in vivo.
RESULTS: Mature TLSs were associated with favorable survival in patients with LCBM, whereas preoperative chemotherapy was independently associated with impaired TLS maturation after adjustment for measured clinical and treatment covariates. Consistent with this observation, chemotherapy-treated tumors exhibited increased IL-33 levels, mast-cell accumulation, and enhanced mast-cell degranulation, with spatial analyses placing activated mast cells within TLS-associated immune niches. Mechanistically, IL-33 activated ST2-expressing mast cells, induced NF-κB-associated transcriptional programs. Functionally, mast cell-derived PGD2 suppressed antigen-specific CD8⁺ T-cell cytotoxicity and impaired plasma-cell differentiation, providing a potential link between chemotherapy and weakened local adaptive immunity. In vivo, pharmacological inhibition of H-PGDS enhanced chemotherapy efficacy, while mast-cell stabilization with cromolyn sodium reduced chemotherapy-associated degranulation, increased local adaptive immune-cell infiltration, improved intracranial tumor control, and prolonged survival when combined with chemotherapy.
CONCLUSIONS: Our findings support a model in which chemotherapy-associated tissue stress activates an IL-33-responsive mast-cell program and restrains TLS-associated adaptive immunity in LCBM. Although this pathway is unlikely to operate as an exclusively linear or mast-cell-specific mechanism, targeting mast-cell-associated inflammatory signaling may provide a strategy to preserve antitumor immunity and improve the therapeutic efficacy of chemotherapy in brain metastasis.

- 作者: Long Z, Wu R, Yuan S, Yang K, Cheng T, Han B, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 18
- DOI: 10.1186/s40364-026-01046-3
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43053160/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-20

#### 摘要

Myelodysplastic neoplasms (MDS) are clonal myeloid neoplasms characterized by ineffective hematopoiesis, cytopenias, and a variable risk of transformation to Acute myeloid leukemia (AML). Although genetic lesions are central to MDS pathogenesis, progression to AML cannot be explained by mutation accumulation alone. This review synthesizes evidence that MDS-to-AML transformation is a dynamic ecological process driven by reciprocal interactions between mutant hematopoietic stem and progenitor cell clones and the bone marrow immune microenvironment. In normal marrow, hematopoietic stem and progenitor cells, T cells, NK(natural killer) cells, myeloid cells, mesenchymal stromal cells, endothelial cells, and the extracellular matrix maintain hematopoietic homeostasis and immune surveillance. In MDS, chronic inflammation coexists with impaired immune effector function, including T-cell exhaustion, reduced NK-cell cytotoxicity, expansion of myeloid-derived suppressor cells and immunosuppressive regulatory T-cell subsets, monocyte-macrophage reprogramming, mesenchymal stromal cell dysfunction, extracellular matrix remodeling, and metabolic adaptation. Together, these alterations establish a proinflammatory but immunosuppressive niche that weakens clearance of abnormal clones while providing stromal and metabolic support for clonal expansion and treatment resistance. Mutant clones further remodel the niche through epigenetic silencing, altered chemokine and cytokine signaling, and inflammatory-metabolic reprogramming, generating positive-feedback circuits that link inflammation, DNA damage, immune escape, increasing clonal burden, and therapy resistance. This review discusses how integrating clonal genetics with immune, stromal, and metabolic features may refine risk stratification and inform microenvironment-directed combination therapies. Longitudinal single-cell and spatial profiling will be essential for identifying reversible immune dysfunction and developing individualized strategies to delay or prevent AML transformation.

### [C] A Dual-Responsive Senolytic Hydrogel Remodels the Postsurgical Senescent Microenvironment to Suppress Glioblastoma Recurrence.

- PMID: 43052017

- 作者: Ning L, Shao Y, You C, Qian Y, Yu H, Yang S, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 18
- DOI: 10.1002/advs.78827
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43052017/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-20

#### 摘要

Tumor recurrence remains the predominant cause of mortality in patients with glioblastoma (GBM), and (chemo/radio-) therapy-induced senescence (TIS) has recently been recognized as a key driver by deteriorating the post-surgical microenvironment. To exploit this "senescence trap", we developed an injectable, bacterial cellulose (BC)-based hydrogel system (ABT@Gal-gel) designed for preferential elimination of senescent GBM cells. This platform encapsulates galactose-modified polydopamine nanoparticles loaded with the senolytic agent ABT-263 (ABT@Gal-PDA). It enables programmed, β-galactosidase (β-Gal)-responsive drug release to preferentially eliminate pro-tumorigenic senescent cells. Moreover, the cellulose and chitosan in the hydrogel further remodel the immunosuppressive tumor microenvironment by inducing M1 macrophage polarization. In orthotopic GBM mouse models, this dual-functional strategy significantly inhibits tumor recurrence and prolongs overall survival. Overall, this study presents a novel strategy by coupling senescence-associated intracellular targeting with tumor-associated macrophage (TAM) repolarization to suppress GBM relapses, providing a promising direction for the next generation of postoperative brain cancer therapies.

### [C] The Benefits of Physical Exercise on Epilepsy Treatment: Possible Mechanisms via Neurotransmitters Modulation.

- PMID: 43062894
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low
- 转化相关性依据: Only generic or out-of-scope keyword overlap

- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: The International journal of neuroscience
- 作者: Taherkhani S, Bagheri Tadi F, Amani P, Mohajerian R, Janzadeh A
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 20
- DOI: 10.1080/00207454.2026.2766974
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43062894/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-21

#### 摘要

This narrative review aims to critically explored current preclinical and clinical evidence on the therapeutic benefits of physical exercise in epilepsy management, with particular emphasis on the underlying mechanisms mediated by modulation of key neurotransmitter systems, including GABAergic, glutamatergic, monoaminergic, and neurotrophic pathways. It seeks to reframe exercise from a historically perceived risk factor to a promising non-pharmacological adjunct capable of reducing seizure susceptibility and improving comorbid outcomes. A comprehensive narrative synthesis of the literature was conducted by searching major databases (PubMed/MEDLINE, Scopus, Web of Science) for peer-reviewed English-language studies examining physical exercise or activity in experimental epilepsy models and people with epilepsy. Inclusion prioritized investigations of neurotransmitter alterations (GABA, glutamate, dopamine, serotonin, noradrenaline), BDNF signaling, neuroinflammation, oxidative stress, and related clinical endpoints such as seizure frequency, cognition, and quality of life. Accumulating evidence demonstrates that regular moderate-to-vigorous aerobic and resistance exercise reduces seizure frequency and severity in animal models and selected clinical cohorts, while concurrently ameliorating depression, anxiety, cognitive deficits, and overall quality of life. Mechanistically, exercise elevates brain GABA levels and GABAergic neurosteroids, restores excitatory-inhibitory balance by modulating glutamate, enhances monoaminergic transmission (dopamine, serotonin, noradrenaline), and robustly upregulates hippocampal BDNF and downstream plasticity pathways (ERK/mTOR). Physical exercise emerges as a safe, accessible, and multifaceted complementary strategy in epilepsy treatment, exerting anticonvulsant and neuroprotective actions principally through neurotransmitter modulation and neurotrophic support. Integration of structured exercise into multidisciplinary care is warranted, although larger randomized trials are needed to optimize intensity, modality, and patient stratification, particularly in drug-resistant epilepsy.

### [C] Stem Cell and Extracellular Vesicle Therapies for Subarachnoid Hemorrhage: Pathophysiology, Preclinical Evidence, and Translational Challenges.

- PMID: 43062740
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low
- 转化相关性依据: Only generic or out-of-scope keyword overlap
- 优先级依据: C: broad, exploratory, low-relevance, or unmatched article
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Translational stroke research
- 作者: Hamad N, Altabbakh O, O'Leary S, Azhar M, Durrani A, Roach C, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 20
- DOI: 10.1007/s12975-026-01506-w
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43062740/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-21

#### 摘要

Subarachnoid hemorrhage (SAH) remains a devastating neurological condition with high mortality and morbidity, largely due to secondary brain injury mechanisms such as early brain injury (EBI) and secondary white matter injury (SWMI). Conventional surgical and medical interventions have not been sufficient to prevent long-term disability, prompting investigation into novel regenerative strategies. We conducted a narrative review of preclinical and limited clinical evidence on stem cell- and extracellular vesicle/exosome-based therapies for SAH, with emphasis on MSCs and their derivatives. PubMed/MEDLINE and Embase were searched between February and March 2025 for studies examining mechanisms, therapeutic targets, dosing, timing, and routes of administration. Stem cell-based therapies, particularly MSCs and their exosomes, have shown the ability to attenuate neuroinflammation, oxidative stress, excitotoxicity, and BBB disruption. Key mechanisms include modulation of the HMGB1/TLR4/NF-κB axis, polarization of microglia toward an anti-inflammatory phenotype, and secretion of trophic factors such as TSG-6, BDNF, and NT-3. Preclinical studies demonstrate improvements in neurological function, cognitive outcomes, and histological preservation across multiple SAH models. Exosome delivery of microRNAs (e.g., miR-129-5p, miR-193b-3p) has emerged as a potent cell-free strategy. Benefits have been reported after both early and delayed administration; however, differences in experimental models, therapeutic products, dosing methods, routes of administration, and outcome measures prevent direct cross-study comparisons and preclude identification of an optimal treatment window. MSC-derived therapies, hold significant promise for mitigating secondary injury and improving outcomes after SAH. Despite this promise, significant translational challenges concerning optimal dosing, delivery methods, and long-term safety must still be addressed. The path to clinical use requires rigorous validation in large animal models before advancing to human trials. Such validation may support the future clinical development of stem cell-based therapies for SAH.

- DOI: 10.1016/j.isci.2026.118176
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43060805/

#### 摘要

Polyunsaturated fatty acids (PUFAs) influence membrane structure and function, energy metabolism, inflammation, and the ability to regulate microglial activity. We show involvement of astrocytes in long-chain PUFA metabolism in human induced pluripotent stem cell (hiPSC)-derived astrocytes with fragile X syndrome (FXS). FXS astrocytes secreted more n-6 docosapentaenoic acid (n-6 DPA) and displayed a higher n-3 DPA to docosahexaenoic acid (DHA) ratio in medium than controls, consistent with an altered n-6 to n-3 PUFA balance in plasma from the FXS mouse. The FXS-specific increase in n-6 DPA secretion was abolished by treatment with all-trans retinoic acid (ATRA), which affected gene expression in astrocyte subpopulations and modulated secreted triacylglycerol species. Microglial exposure to n-6 DPA stimulated expression of brain-derived neurotrophic factor (BDNF), which also tended to increase in microglia with reduced inflammatory marker expression when cultured in medium from ATRA-treated astrocytes. The results indicate that astrocyte-mediated n-6 DPA regulates the trophic functions of microglia in FXS.

### [C] rTMS Attenuates Microglial Phagocytosis and Promotes Neurorepair in a Photothrombotic Stroke Mouse Model.

- PMID: 43059117
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low
- 转化相关性依据: Only generic or out-of-scope keyword overlap
- 优先级依据: C: broad, exploratory, low-relevance, or unmatched article
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: The International journal of neuroscience
- 作者: Han Y, Tuerhong B, Zhang Y, Wu J, Mo L
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 19
- DOI: 10.1080/00207454.2026.2766977
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43059117/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-21

#### 摘要

OBJECTIVE: This study investigated the effects of repetitive transcranial magnetic stimulation (rTMS) on functional recovery in photothrombotic (PT) stroke mice and whether rTMS promotes neurorepair by modulating microglial phagocytosis.
METHODS: Eight-week-old male C57BL/6J mice were used to establish a focal stroke model via photothrombosis (PT group). The Sham group and PT + rTMS group were included. rTMS was initiated 24 h after modeling. Neurological deficits were assessed. Microglial activation, synaptic phagocytosis, neuronal injury, and the levels of tumor necrosis factor-alpha (TNF-α), interleukin-6 (IL-6), and interleukin-1β (IL-1β) were evaluated. Differentially expressed proteins and core regulatory pathways were analyzed.
RESULTS: rTMS treatment significantly increased body weight, prolonged the latency to fall and shortened the balance beam crossing time, reduced foot faults, and improved the discrimination index in PT mice. rTMS reduced the number of Iba1-positive microglia and CD68-positive cells, attenuated microglial synaptic phagocytosis, and alleviated neuronal loss and apoptosis. rTMS significantly decreased the levels of TNF-α, IL-6, and IL-1β in the brain. A total of 416 differentially expressed proteins were identified between the PT and PT + rTMS groups. The differentially expressed proteins were significantly enriched in microglial phagocytosis pathways and related signaling pathways.
CONCLUSION: rTMS significantly ameliorates neurological deficits and cognitive impairment in PT stroke mice. The underlying mechanism may involve the inhibition of excessive microglial activation, attenuation of microglia-mediated synaptic phagocytosis, and alleviation of neuroinflammation. Proteomic analysis reveals that rTMS modulates the expression of proteins associated with microglial phagocytosis-related signaling pathways, which may contribute to the observed post-stroke neurorepair effects.

### [C] Glioblastoma inflammatory tumor microenvironment: Caspase-mediated pyroptosis as target to break immunosuppression and therapy resistance.

- 作者: Barocci G, Cirotti C, Barilà D, Contadini C
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 19

- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43057431/

#### 摘要

Glioblastoma (GB) is the most aggressive primary brain tumor with still no curative therapy available. One major obstacle to therapy efficacy is the presence of a complex immunosuppressive tumor microenvironment (TME). Pyroptosis is an inflammatory cell death that can be either productive or abortive, thereby playing opposite effects in tumor progression. While acute productive pyroptosis triggers an anti-tumor immune response, abortive pyroptosis may contribute to a low and chronic inflammation, which finally sustains immunosuppression. This review examines how abortive pyroptosis reshapes the GB TME, emphasizing the potential role of caspase-8, and discusses the novel therapeutic strategies aimed at restoring productive immunogenic pyroptosis to reactivate the anti-tumor immune response.

### [C] Targeting Microglial Senescence in Brain Ageing and Alzheimer's Disease: Mechanistic Evidence and the Therapeutic Potential of Natural Products.

- PMID: 43057397
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low
- 转化相关性依据: Only generic or out-of-scope keyword overlap
- 优先级依据: C: broad, exploratory, low-relevance, or unmatched article
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Ageing research reviews
- 作者: Alok S
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 19
- DOI: 10.1016/j.arr.2026.103449
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43057397/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-21

#### 摘要

Microglial senescence is a candidate contributor to brain ageing and Alzheimer's disease, but its identification and therapeutic tractability remain contested. Activation, lysosomal expansion, lipid accumulation and dystrophic morphology do not independently establish senescence. This review critically examines how uncertainty in state identification affects mechanistic interpretation and natural-product evaluation. Genetic perturbation, human induced pluripotent stem cell models and pharmacological studies connect mitochondrial proteotoxic stress, impaired autophagy, DNA damage and sustained inflammatory signalling with senescence-associated microglial phenotypes. Disease-associated and senescence-associated microglial populations are not interchangeable. Delphinidin, andrographolide, resveratrol, honokiol, rosmarinic acid and cycloastragenol reduce senescence-associated readouts in selected experimental systems. Current evidence mainly supports prevention or phenotype modulation; selective microglial senolysis, durable reversal and senescence-dependent cognitive rescue remain unestablished. Functional assessment requires substrate specificity: impaired harmful-substrate disposal can coexist with excessive synaptic engulfment. Translation requires achievable unbound brain exposure, treatment after phenotype establishment and models relevant to human ageing. Circulating inflammatory-marker changes alone cannot demonstrate drug action within microglia or modification of their senescence-associated state. Small, uncontrolled senolytic trials support the feasibility of treatment administration and preliminary pharmacological assessment, but do not establish cognitive efficacy or selective action on senescent microglia. Progress depends on convergent state identification, delayed intervention, lineage-resolved causal experiments and exposure-matched pharmacology. Natural products remain useful mechanistic probes and potential therapeutic leads whose value should be judged by restoration of protective microglial functions and neural outcomes, rather than marker suppression alone.

### [C] Hepatic Macrophages Depletion by Clodronate Liposomes Alleviates RRV-Induced Biliary Injury via Targeting the Notch3/PPAR Signalling Pathway.

- PMID: 43065273

- 作者: Chang X, Bai X, Ning Y, Liang J, Liang Q, Wen Z
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 20
- DOI: 10.1111/imm.70227
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43065273/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-22

#### 摘要

Biliary atresia is a rare obliterative cholangiopathy in neonates, and hepatic macrophages participate in biliary atresia pathogenesis. The study aimed to reveal the regulatory mechanism of macrophages in the progression of biliary atresia. Hepatic macrophages were co-cultured with vascular smooth muscle cells (VSMCs). Delta like canonical notch ligand 4 (DLL4) and neurogenic locus notch homologue 3 (Notch3) were quantified in rhesus rotavirus (RRV)-treated cells following anti-DLL4 antibody REGN1035. In RRV-challenged mice, Notch3 was activated by Ade-NICD3, while macrophages were depleted by clodronate liposomes (Clo-Lip) to compare intervention effects on RRV-induced biliary injury, hypoxia inducible factor-1α (HIF-1α), Notch3, CD68 and CD163. Notch3 knockout mice were used to verify the effect of Notch3 on RRV-induced injury. Transcriptome sequencing screened Notch3-related differentially expressed genes, and downstream pathway regulation was further verified. Macrophages upregulated DLL4, while REGN1035 treatment inhibited DLL4 and Notch3 expression. In RRV-infected mice, Clo-Lip-mediated macrophage depletion alleviated biliary injury, repressed Notch3 signalling and downregulated HIF-1α, CD68 and CD163. In RRV mice with NICD3 overexpression, macrophage depletion alleviated Notch3 activation-triggered biliary lesions, inhibited inflammation and downregulated HIF-1α, CD68 and CD163. Transcriptomic analysis identified 715 upregulated and 585 downregulated genes, confirming the peroxisome proliferators-activated receptor (PPAR) signalling pathway as a downstream pathway of Notch3. Compared with RRV-infected wild-type mice, Notch3 knockout alleviated RRV-induced biliary injury and abnormal macrophage polarization, downregulated HIF-1α and CYP7A1, but upregulated PPARα. This protective effect was abolished by the PPARα inhibitor GW6471. Macrophage depletion ameliorates RRV-induced biliary injury via regulating the Notch3/PPAR signalling pathway.

#### 摘要

Strategies to provide less-differentiated naive T cell clones in patients with cancer and immunodeficiencies are expected to improve the efficacy of immunotherapies. Adoptive transfer of T cell progenitors with thymus-seeding progenitors (TSPs) differentiated from human induced pluripotent stem cells (iPS cells) represents a potential approach to provide histocompatible naive T cell clones. In contrast to mature T cells, the generation of TSPs from human iPS cells remained a challenge. Here, we identified a chemically defined condition to generate VE-cadherin+CD34hiCD7med TSP-like cells (iTSPs) from human iPS cell-derived hemogenic endothelial cells (HECs). The differentiated cells engrafted into the thymus of immunodeficient mice and yielded CCR7+CD45RA+CD62L+TCF7+ naive T cells expressing a diverse T cell receptor (TCR) repertoire. iTSPs expressing a CD19-targeting chimeric antigen receptor (CAR) differentiated into CAR T cells in recipients and mediated antitumor activity against CD19+ leukemia. Together, these findings establish a platform for generating human iTSPs and provide developmental insight into naive-like T cell generation in vivo.

### [C] Inhibition of NLRP3 maintains microglial architecture and enhances neurological recovery after traumatic brain injury.

- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 20
- DOI: 10.1016/j.xcrm.2026.103179
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/43063935/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-22

#### 摘要

The NLR family pyrin domain containing protein 3 (NLRP3) inflammasome orchestrates neuroinflammation, but its role in traumatic brain injury (TBI) and the therapeutic value of pharmacological inhibition remain incompletely defined. Using Nlrp3-deficient mice and two novel orally bioavailable selective antagonists (NP3-253 and NP3-361), we investigate NLRP3-dependent mechanisms of post-traumatic neuroinflammation and functional recovery. After controlled cortical impact, NLRP3 inflammasome components, pro-inflammatory genes, and mediators are robustly upregulated during the first post-injury week. Genetic Nlrp3 deletion and post-TBI NP3-253 treatment preserve microglial architecture and reduce aggregation and redistribution of the inflammasome adaptor apoptosis-associated speck-like protein containing a CARD (ASC), while post-TBI NP3-253 and NP3-361 therapeutically accelerate neurological recovery during treatment. Longitudinal intravital imaging shows that pre-TBI NLRP3 inhibition with NP3-361 constrains post-traumatic microglial activation in vivo. These findings establish NLRP3 as a driver of post-traumatic microglial morphodynamics and support selective NLRP3 inhibition as a promising strategy to improve neurological outcomes after TBI.

### [C] Shenghui decoction improves sleep-deprivation-induced cognitive impairment in mice by modulating neuroinflammation, NRG1/ErbB4 signaling, and purine metabolism.

- PMID: 42805352
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low
- 转化相关性依据: Only generic or out-of-scope keyword overlap
- 优先级依据: C: broad, exploratory, low-relevance, or unmatched article
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 近期补位
- 期刊: Journal of ethnopharmacology
- 作者: Lei Y, Tan J, Zhang M, Zhang S, Xia J, Yin C, et al.
- 发表日期: 2027 Feb 10
- DOI: 10.1016/j.jep.2026.122412
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42805352/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-22

#### 摘要

ETHNOPHARMACOLOGICAL RELEVANCE: Shenghui Decoction (SHD) is a traditional Chinese medicine formula first documented by Chen Shiduo in Bian Zheng Lu during the Qing dynasty. It has traditionally been used for disorders related to forgetfulness, insomnia, and mental disturbance. Cognitive impairment induced by sleep deprivation (SD) shares features with these traditional indications. However, the mechanisms underlying the protective effects of SHD against SD-induced cognitive impairment remain unclear.
OBJECTIVE: This study aimed to evaluate the protective effects of SHD against SD-induced cognitive impairment in mice and investigate potential mechanisms involving neuroinflammation, NRG1/ErbB4-related synaptic plasticity, and metabolic homeostasis.
MATERIALS AND METHODS: Male C57BL/6 mice were randomly assigned to five groups: the negative control group (Control), SD model group (Model), melatonin group (MT, 100 mg/kg), low-dose SHD group (SHD-L, 13.5 g/kg), and high-dose SHD group (SHD-H, 27 g/kg). Mice received intragastric treatment once daily for 21 consecutive days, and 72-h SD was initiated on day 18 using a modified multiple-platform water-environment method. Cognitive function was evaluated using the novel object recognition test and Morris water maze. Hippocampal histopathology was assessed by hematoxylin and eosin and Nissl staining. Western blotting was used to quantify hippocampal levels of interleukin-1β (IL-1β), interleukin-6 (IL-6), tumor necrosis factor-α (TNF-α), NRG1, ErbB4, phosphorylated ErbB4 (p-ErbB4), parvalbumin (PV), postsynaptic density protein 95 (PSD95), and brain-derived neurotrophic factor (BDNF). Network pharmacology analysis was performed using the 15 blood-absorbed prototype constituents of SHD identified in our previous study. Untargeted serum metabolomics was subsequently conducted using ultra-high-performance liquid chromatography coupled with quadrupole time-of-flight tandem mass spectrometry. Finally, network pharmacology and metabolomics data were integrated for joint analysis.
RESULTS: SD significantly impaired recognition and spatial learning and memory. These behavioral deficits were accompanied by marked histopathological changes in the hippocampal dentate gyrus and cornu ammonis 3 regions. SHD-H significantly ameliorated both the behavioral and histopathological abnormalities. SD also increased hippocampal IL-1β, IL-6, and TNF-α levels, while reducing NRG1, PV, PSD95, and BDNF levels and the p-ErbB4/ErbB4 ratio. SHD-H reversed these changes to varying degrees. Network pharmacology identified 88 core targets. Kyoto Encyclopedia of Genes and Genomes enrichment analysis highlighted Ras signaling pathways, PI3K-Akt signaling pathways, MAPK signaling pathways, and ErbB signaling pathways. Untargeted serum metabolomics identified 48 differential metabolites altered by SD. Pathway enrichment analysis showed that these metabolites were mainly associated with glycerophospholipid metabolism, sphingolipid metabolism, and purine metabolism. SHD-H shifted these metabolic alterations toward the Control profile to varying degrees. A MetScape-based metabolite-reaction-enzyme-gene network was constructed to identify genes associated with the 48 differential metabolites. Intersecting these metabolite-associated genes with the 88 core network pharmacology targets yielded four candidate overlapping targets: APRT, NT5M, ADK, and HPRT1. Further analysis of the metabolite-reaction-enzyme-gene network linked all four candidate targets to deoxyguanosine-related metabolic reactions. The four candidate targets were then mapped back to the predicted target profiles of the 15 blood-absorbed prototype constituents of SHD. This analysis identified morroniside, loganin, and ginsenoside Rb1 as blood-absorbed prototype constituents predicted to be associated with one or more of these candidate targets. Molecular docking further suggested favorable potential interactions between these constituents and their corresponding candidate targets.
CONCLUSION: SHD ameliorated SD-induced cognitive impairment and hippocampal histopathological damage in mice. These effects were associated with reduced hippocampal neuroinflammation and partial normalization of NRG1/ErbB4-related PV and synaptic plasticity markers. Integrated network pharmacology and metabolomics analyses further suggest that purine metabolism may represent a potential link between SD-induced systemic metabolic disturbances and the effects of SHD. These findings provide experimental evidence for the pharmacological basis of the traditional use of SHD for conditions associated with forgetfulness, insomnia, and mental disturbance.

### [C] Targeting glial glycolysis with EGCG alleviates Parkinson's disease in a PINK1 mutant Drosophila model.

- PMID: 42785532
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence

- 期刊: Journal of ethnopharmacology
- 作者: Jiang R, Huang Y, Liu ZH, Wang HL
- 发表日期: 2027 Feb 10
- DOI: 10.1016/j.jep.2026.122405
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42785532/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-22

#### 摘要

ETHNOPHARMACOLOGICAL RELEVANCE: Camellia sinensis (L.) Kuntze (Theaceae), commonly known as green tea, has been traditionally utilized in Traditional Chinese Medicine (TCM) as a neurotonic to clear the mind, alleviate fatigue, and counteract aging-related cognitive decline. Epigallocatechin-3-gallate (EGCG), its principal bioactive polyphenol, exhibits well-documented neuroprotective properties, providing a modern pharmacological rationale for its traditional botanical application in managing neurological disorders such as Parkinson's disease (PD).
AIM OF THE STUDY: While the neuroprotective effects of EGCG against PD are known, whether its therapeutic efficacy involves the modulation of brain glucose metabolism remains largely unexplored. This study aimed to investigate the specific alterations of glucose metabolism in a PINK1 mutant Drosophila model of PD and to elucidate the role of EGCG in regulating glial glycolysis and subsequent neuroinflammation.
MATERIALS AND METHODS: A Drosophila melanogaster model of PD carrying a PINK1 mutation was utilized. The metabolic shift in the brain was evaluated using quantitative PCR and biochemical assays. Pharmacological interventions (using EGCG, the glycolysis inhibitor oxamate, and exogenous lactate) and bidirectional genetic manipulations (glia-specific knockdown or overexpression of lactate dehydrogenase LDH via the Repo-GAL4 driver) were employed. Treatment outcomes were comprehensively assessed through behavioral assays (climbing and locomotor activity tracking), immunohistochemical quantification of dopaminergic neurons, and transcriptional analysis of inflammatory markers.
RESULTS: We identified a prominent metabolic shift from oxidative phosphorylation (OXPHOS) toward glycolysis in the PD Drosophila brain, accompanied by aberrant lactate accumulation. Pharmacological or genetic inhibition of this glial glycolysis alleviated both neuronal and behavioral deficits. Conversely, overactivation of the glial glycolytic pathway (LDH overexpression) exacerbated these phenotypes and triggered neuroinflammation. Crucially, pharmacological intervention with EGCG effectively downregulated the expression of key glycolytic enzymes, restored OXPHOS capacity, significantly suppressed glial-driven neuroinflammation, and attenuated a broad spectrum of PD-associated impairments.
CONCLUSIONS: This study shows that EGCG, derived from Camellia sinensis (L.) Kuntze, alleviates PD disease symptoms by modulating glial glucose metabolism and inhibiting the glycolysis-lactate-neuroinflammation axis. These findings provide a novel mechanistic basis for the neuroprotective properties of tea polyphenols. Ultimately, this work provides modern pharmacological validation for the traditional use of green tea in mitigating age-related neurological decline, highlighting glial metabolic modulation by EGCG as a promising therapeutic strategy for neurodegenerative disorders.

### [C] Pain mechanisms in trigeminal neuralgia: From peripheral pathology to brain network reorganization and neurosurgical implications.

- PMID: 42925839
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Exploratory
- 转化相关性依据: Cross-domain vascular mechanism with exploratory CCM relevance
- 优先级依据: C: broad, exploratory, low-relevance, or unmatched article
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 近期补位
- 期刊: Journal of clinical neuroscience : official journal of the Neurosurgical Society of Australasia
- 作者: Serhiienko YH, Nekrasova NO, Piatykop VO, Yu Kaliuzhka V
- 发表日期: 2027 Jan
- DOI: 10.1016/j.jocn.2026.112370
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42925839/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-22

#### 摘要

OBJECTIVES: Trigeminal neuralgia (TN) involves peripheral neurovascular pathology and may also be associated with maladaptive neuroplastic changes in central nervous system networks. This review synthesizes evidence on peripheral and central pain mechanisms, neuroimaging biomarkers, molecular and transcriptomic correlates, and neurosurgical implications, including emerging neuromodulation strategies.
MATERIALS AND METHODS: This narrative review drew on topic-oriented searches of PubMed/MEDLINE, Scopus, and Web of Science conducted between June 27 and July 5, 2026, covering literature published through June 2026. Supplementary targeted searches and reference-list screening were used to identify relevant publications. Evidence was synthesized qualitatively.
RESULTS: Evidence supports a multilevel model of TN involving peripheral pathology and central neuroplastic changes. Neuroimaging studies report structural and functional alterations in sensorimotor, salience, limbic, and default mode networks, alongside disrupted structure-function coupling. Partial restoration of functional connectivity after neurosurgical treatment has been reported, although outcomes vary. Transcriptomic findings link imaging abnormalities to genes involved in ion channel function, synaptic signaling, neuroinflammation, and energy metabolism but remain hypothesis-generating. Transcutaneous electrical nerve stimulation (TENS) may provide short-term analgesic benefit; however, evidence is limited and heterogeneous.
CONCLUSIONS: TN may be understood as a disorder involving interactions between peripheral pathology and central nervous system reorganization. Integrating neuroimaging and molecular evidence may inform future patient stratification and neurosurgical decision-making. Individualized neuromodulation remains investigational and requires further clinical validation.

### [C] Ineupatorolide B alleviates postpartum depression-like behaviors via the gut microbiota-butyrate-brain axis.

- PMID: 42913991
- Domain: Out-of-scope

- 来源类型: 近期补位
- 期刊: European journal of pharmacology
- 作者: Duan X, Tian F, Liu W, Yang Y, Gao Y, Hashimoto K, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Dec 26
- DOI: 10.1016/j.ejphar.2026.179444
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42913991/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-22

#### 摘要

Postpartum depression (PPD) is a common mood disorder after childbirth and is associated with abrupt hormonal withdrawal, systemic inflammation, and neurobiological dysfunction. Increasing evidence implicates dysregulation of the microbiota-gut-brain axis in PPD pathophysiology, but safe and effective treatments remain limited. Here, we investigated the antidepressant-like effects and mechanisms of ineupatorolide B (InB), a bioactive compound isolated from Inula species, using a hormone-simulated withdrawal mouse model of PPD. InB significantly alleviated depressive- and anhedonia-like behaviors, as shown by reduced immobility in the tail suspension and forced swim tests and restored sucrose preference, with efficacy comparable to fluoxetine. InB also reduced serum interleukin-6 and tumor necrosis factor-α levels and restored the expression of postsynaptic density protein 95 and brain-derived neurotrophic factor in the prefrontal cortex. Gut microbiota analysis revealed marked alterations in microbial diversity and composition in hormone-withdrawal mice, accompanied by reduced fecal butyrate levels, which were largely normalized by InB treatment. Correlation analyses suggested associations among specific bacterial phyla, short-chain fatty acid metabolism, and behavioral outcomes. Notably, oral administration of butyric acid also alleviated depressive-like behaviors, suppressed systemic inflammation, and restored PSD-95, BDNF, and occludin expression in the prefrontal cortex of hormone-withdrawal mice. Together, these findings suggest that InB ameliorates PPD-like phenotypes, possibly through modulation of the gut microbiota, butyrate metabolism, systemic inflammation, and synaptic plasticity. However, the causal relationships among these changes remain to be established.

### [C] Laminarin attenuates cerebral ischemia-reperfusion injury by inhibiting Notch1 signaling and reprogramming microglial inflammatory responses.

- PMID: 42911059
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low

- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 近期补位
- 期刊: Neuroreport
- 作者: Chen D, Xie J, Wu X, Kong D, Luo J
- 发表日期: 2026 Dec 16
- DOI: 10.1097/WNR.0000000000002301
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42911059/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-22

#### 摘要

BACKGROUND: Neuroinflammation contributes to cerebral ischemia-reperfusion injury, and microglial inflammatory responses are regulated in part by Notch signaling. This study investigated whether laminarin, a brown algae-derived β-glucan polysaccharide, protects against cerebral ischemia-reperfusion injury by modulating Notch1-dependent microglial responses.
METHODS: A rat middle cerebral artery occlusion/reperfusion model was established, and laminarin was administered intraperitoneally at 10 mg/kg for seven consecutive days. Neurological deficits and infarct volume were assessed by modified neurological severity score and 2,3,5-triphenyltetrazolium chloride staining. Transcriptomic and bioinformatic analyses were used to identify laminarin-associated pathways. BV2 microglial cells were stimulated with lipopolysaccharide, and Jagged1 was used to reactivate Notch signaling. Inducible nitric oxide synthase (iNOS), Arginase 1 (Arg1), Notch pathway proteins, and inflammatory cytokines were examined by immunofluorescence, western blotting, and enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay.
RESULTS: Laminarin reduced infarct volume and improved neurological recovery in rats. Bioinformatic analysis and immunofluorescence indicated involvement of the Notch pathway. In vivo and in vitro , laminarin decreased iNOS-associated pro-inflammatory activation and increased Arg1-associated reparative responses, accompanied by reduced Notch1/Hes1/Hes5 activation. Jagged1 attenuated these effects and partially reversed laminarin-mediated changes in inflammatory markers and cytokine production.
CONCLUSION: Laminarin attenuates cerebral ischemia-reperfusion injury by reprogramming microglial inflammatory responses, at least partly through inhibition of the Notch1/Hes1/Hes5 signaling pathway. These findings support laminarin as a potential marine-derived immunomodulatory candidate for ischemic stroke research.

### [C] Tetanus toxoid immunoprophylaxis boosts cognitive function by promoting hippocampal neurogenesis and white matter myelination in ageing experimental mice.

- PMID: 42838211
- Domain: Out-of-scope

- 期刊: European journal of pharmacology
- 作者: Babu Deva Irakkam MP, Kandasamy M
- 发表日期: 2026 Dec 10
- DOI: 10.1016/j.ejphar.2026.179393
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42838211/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-22

#### 摘要

Tetanus toxoid (TT), a widely administered prophylactic immunogen, not only provides protection against tetanus but also exerts neuromodulatory effects, for which experimental evidence remains limited. In this study, we investigated the effect of TT on cognitive function in relation to hippocampal neurogenesis, myelination, key markers for neuroinflammation, oxidative stress and cell death in the brain of twelve-month-old mice. The treatment group exhibited higher levels of immunoglobulin gamma (IgG) and increased lymphocyte count in the blood sample. Results from the novel object recognition (NOR) test, cued radial arm maze (cued RAM), and Morris water maze (MWM) revealed that TT administration enhanced learning and memory in ageing mice. These cognitive improvements were associated with an increased number of doublecortin (DCX)-positive immature neurons and a higher proportion of bromodeoxyuridine (BrdU)/neuronal nuclei (NeuN) double-positive new neurons in the hippocampus. In addition, TT treatment resulted in enhanced myelination in the corpus callosum, hippocampal hilus and striatum as evidenced by increased intensity of luxol fast blue (LFB) staining and myelin basic protein (MBP) expression. Moreover, TT treatment induced a phenotypic shift in microglial phenotype from the pro-inflammatory M1 state to the neuroprotective M2 state. TT-treated animals showed a significant reduction in the levels of an oxidative stress marker, malondialdehyde (MDA), and a lower number of terminal deoxynucleotidyl transferase dUTP nick end labeling (TUNEL) cells, suggesting that TT alleviates oxidative stress and prevents neuronal apoptosis. Overall, these findings demonstrate TT as a potential modulator of neuroregeneration, remyelination and neuroprotection, emphasizing its therapeutic potential for enhancing cognitive function.
